Complete reference: 260 terms and shapes · All vocabularies and reading guide

The Binary Data Description Ontology (BDDO) provides a formal vocabulary to declaratively describe the structure, syntax, and layout of binary data. It is the foundational physical layer of the Hexplain Ontology Family. BDDO allows authors to model binary formats as nested structures and typed fields, with support for dynamic layouts through conditional logic, variable sizing, offset-based addressing, bit-level fields, arrays, stream synchronization, enumerations, and integrity checksums. This specification provides the normative OWL vocabulary for BDDO and a set of SHACL shapes for validating BDDO descriptions.

This document is an unofficial draft produced for the purpose of defining the Hexplain standard. It has no official standing and is not endorsed by any standards body.

Introduction

The primary goal of the Hexplain project is to make opaque binary data machine-understandable. The first step is to provide a standardized, unambiguous way to describe the physical structure of the data. The Binary Data Description Ontology (BDDO) serves this purpose.

BDDO provides a set of RDF classes and properties that can be used to create a graph-based model of a binary format. This model can then be used by a conforming Hexplain processor (see the Processing Model specification) to parse a binary stream into a structured, logical representation, and ultimately into an RDF instance graph.

Modeling Approach: Vocabulary vs. Constraints

BDDO follows a deliberate separation of concerns that is a recommended ontology-engineering practice:

  • The OWL vocabulary declares terms (classes, properties, individuals) with human-readable metadata (rdfs:label, rdfs:comment, rdfs:isDefinedBy), lightweight semantics (rdfs:subClassOf, alignment axioms), and each property's rdfs:range (its value type). It deliberately does not assert rdfs:domain, because a domain axiom would silently type any subject of a property — a term is often reused across several classes.
  • The SHACL shapes carry the remaining constraints — the classes a property may apply to (its "domain"), value cardinalities, and any refinement of the declared ranges (controlled-vocabulary membership, numeric bounds). Validation therefore never mutates the graph, and errors are reported rather than repaired by inference.

Conformance

The key words "MUST", "MUST NOT", "REQUIRED", "SHALL", "SHALL NOT", "SHOULD", "SHOULD NOT", "RECOMMENDED", "MAY", and "OPTIONAL" in this document are to be interpreted as described in BCP 14 [[RFC2119]] [[RFC8174]] when, and only when, they appear in all capitals, as shown here.

A validator checking a BDDO description graph against § Normative SHACL Shapes MUST either include the BDDO rdfs:subClassOf axioms of § Normative OWL Vocabulary in the graph it validates, or apply RDFS subclass closure to that graph before validating. SHACL selects the targets of sh:targetClass through rdf:type/rdfs:subClassOf*, so a validator that omits those axioms silently under-validates every subclass: a bddo:KeyValueHeader, for example, would not be reached by bddo:StructShape and its bddo:endianness would go unchecked.

Core Concepts

The BDDO model is built upon the following classes.

bddo:Struct
A composite structure or container for other elements. A Struct is an ordered sequence of Fields. Examples: a file header, a network packet, a PNG chunk.
bddo:Field
A single element within a Struct. A Field may represent one value, a bit-level slice, or an array of values, and is associated with a data type.
bddo:DataType
The type of data a Field contains. BDDO defines core primitive data types (e.g. bddo:uint32be). This class can be extended in profiles to create user-defined type aliases (e.g. media:fourCC).
bddo:DataTypeRule
A single rule for conditionally selecting a data type for a field, consisting of a condition and the data type to use when the condition holds.
bddo:EndiannessRule
A single rule for conditionally selecting a byte order for a Struct or Field, consisting of a condition and the endianness to use when the condition holds. Used by formats that declare their own byte order in a magic or version field.
bddo:Enumeration / bddo:EnumValue
A mapping of raw parsed values to named symbols, so that (for example) a colour-type byte of 2 can be surfaced as RGB in the output graph and referenced by name in conditions.
bddo:Checksum
A declarative description of an integrity check: the algorithm and the byte range it covers. A field that stores a checksum value links to it so that a processor can verify data integrity.

Sequencing, Addressing & Alignment

By default, the Fields listed in bddo:hasField are parsed sequentially and contiguously: each field begins where the previous one ended. Many real formats (TIFF/GeoTIFF IFDs, ZIP central directories, ELF section tables, PDF cross-reference tables) instead locate structures by offset. BDDO supports both models.

bddo:atOffset, bddo:atOffsetFromField, bddo:atOffsetFromExpression
Relocate a Field or Struct to a fixed offset, an offset read from another field, or an offset computed by a HEL expression, respectively. A field with an offset does not advance the sequential cursor for its siblings unless the processor is told otherwise.
bddo:offsetBase
The reference point the offset is measured from: bddo:streamStart (default), bddo:streamEnd (for formats parsed from the tail, such as ZIP), bddo:parentStart, or bddo:currentPosition.
bddo:alignment
An xsd:positiveInteger. Before parsing the field, the processor MUST advance the cursor to the next multiple of this value (padding bytes are skipped).

Bit-Level Fields

Formats such as IPv4, TCP, and many compression headers pack several logical values into a single byte. A Field MAY declare bddo:bitLength (an xsd:positiveInteger number of bits). Consecutive bit-length fields are packed into the underlying bytes; the packing order is governed by bddo:bitOrder (bddo:MSBFirst, the default, or bddo:LSBFirst) declared on the enclosing Struct. A run of bit fields SHOULD align to a byte boundary; a processor MUST pad to the next byte before parsing a subsequent non-bit field.

Delimited Text Records

Record- and line-oriented text — key = value headers, CSV, whitespace-separated grids — cannot be addressed by byte offset. bddo:DelimitedRecords is a bddo:Struct whose children are located by delimiter position instead: bddo:recordDelimiter splits the byte run into records, and bddo:fieldDelimiter splits each record into values. Its two specialisations cover the common cases — bddo:KeyValueHeader, where each field is matched by bddo:key rather than position, and bddo:DelimitedTable, where bddo:hasField gives an ordered column list that repeats per row.

A field of a bddo:DelimitedRecords takes its extent from its container's delimiters, so it declares no bddo:size and no bddo:terminator of its own; restating a delimiter as a field's terminator is redundant and breaks when a column is inserted. Such fields are therefore exempt from the sizing requirements that apply to variable-length and ASCII-coded numeric fields elsewhere.

Column-aligned text needs a different separation rule. In an ASCII grid a key and its value, or two adjacent numbers, are parted by a run of spaces whose width exists only to line the columns up — ncols 100. A fixed bddo:fieldDelimiter would read each of those spaces as another separator and yield a row of empty values, so bddo:whitespaceSeparated says instead that a run of one or more whitespace bytes, of any length, separates exactly two values. Leading and trailing whitespace on a record is ignored. The two rules are mutually exclusive.

Omitting bddo:recordDelimiter as well makes the content one flat stream of values rather than rows. That is how an ASCII grid whose rows wrap freely across lines is read: line breaks carry no meaning, and the grid's shape comes from the field's dlv:DataLayout or repeat count.

This primitive is deliberately non-recursive. It describes flat delimited text only; nested grammars such as XML and JSON are outside BDDO's scope by design.

Core Data Types

BDDO provides a normative set of built-in primitive data types. All conforming processors MUST support these types. Each is an instance of bddo:DataType and carries a normative bddo:xsdType that fixes the RDF literal datatype produced when a value of that type is emitted into the output graph.

Integer and floating types come in three forms: an endianness-neutral form (e.g. bddo:uint32) that inherits its byte order from the enclosing Struct or Field via bddo:endianness, and explicit big-endian / little-endian forms (e.g. bddo:uint32be, bddo:uint32le). Single-byte types have no byte order. Widths are 8, 16, 24, 32 and 64 bits for integers and 16, 32 and 64 bits for floats: the three-byte integers and the IEEE half-precision float are as common in media, sensor and scientific formats (WAV and AIFF 24-bit PCM, OpenEXR and TIFF half floats, glTF and NumPy float16) as the power-of-two sizes.

Integer Types (excerpt)

IRIbitWidthisSignedendiannessxsdType
bddo:uint8 / bddo:int88false / true—xsd:unsignedByte / xsd:byte
bddo:uint16 / bddo:uint16be / bddo:uint16le16falseinherited / BE / LExsd:unsignedShort
bddo:uint24 / bddo:uint24be / bddo:uint24le24falseinherited / BE / LExsd:unsignedInt
bddo:uint32 / bddo:uint32be / bddo:uint32le32falseinherited / BE / LExsd:unsignedInt
bddo:uint64 / bddo:uint64be / bddo:uint64le64falseinherited / BE / LExsd:unsignedLong
bddo:int16…, bddo:int24…, bddo:int32…, bddo:int64…16/24/32/64trueinherited / BE / LExsd:short/int/int/long

Floating-Point & Other Types

IRIDescriptionxsdType
bddo:float16/be/leIEEE 754 binary16 (half precision)xsd:float
bddo:float32/be/leIEEE 754 32-bit floatxsd:float
bddo:float64/be/leIEEE 754 64-bit floatxsd:double
bddo:bytesRaw byte array. Requires a sizing or termination property.xsd:hexBinary
bddo:stringText. Requires a sizing or termination property; MAY have bddo:encoding.xsd:string

Three-byte integers and half floats. A processor MUST read a bddo:uint24 as the unsigned value of its three bytes in the effective byte order (0 to 16,777,215), and a bddo:int24 as the same 24 bits read as a two's-complement integer (−8,388,608 to 8,388,607): FF FF FE big-endian is −2, never 16,777,214. It MUST read a bddo:float16 as an IEEE 754 binary16 value — one sign bit, five exponent bits with bias 15, ten fraction bits — including subnormals (exponent field 0: the fraction times 2−24), both zeros, both infinities and NaN, and its value is the exact binary16 value as a HEL Float, with no rounding, since every binary16 value is exactly a binary64 one: 3C 00 is 1.0, 00 01 is 2−24, 7B FF is 65504, 7C 00 is +Infinity. Neither type is aligned or padded: each occupies exactly bitWidth / 8 bytes wherever it stands. Emitted, the 24-bit integers take the 32-bit XSD types (xsd:unsignedInt, xsd:int) and bddo:float16 takes xsd:float, all of which hold every value of the narrower type exactly.

Tree Documents

A great many formats wrap a payload that BDDO can already describe in a label it could not read at all: PDS4, DIMAP, SAFE, Sentinel-2, RadarSat-2, TerraSAR-X and CPHD are all an XML manifest over a clean binary array, and Zarr's chunk store was fully covered by chunked layout and encoding pipelines while .zarray — being JSON — kept it out of reach. A bddo:TreeDocument is a Struct whose fields are located by a path into a parsed node tree.

BDDO does not describe the syntax of these documents. It addresses nodes in the tree a conforming parser produces. That distinction is what keeps this from turning into an XML specification: the tree is someone else's output, and BDDO's only job is to say which node supplies which field.

bddo:treeSyntaxbddo:nodePath is…Example
bddo:xmlAn abbreviated XPath: child steps, attribute steps, positional predicates (1-based). No axes, no functions, no comparison predicates./pds:Product/pds:Array_2D_Image/pds:Axis_Array[1]/pds:elements
bddo:jsonA JSON Pointer [[RFC6901]]: member names and array indices (0-based)./shape/0, /compressor/id

Both are adopted rather than invented. JSON Pointer already specifies how to escape / and ~ inside a member name, and is already implemented everywhere; the XPath subset is deliberately small because a description states where a value is, and a path that can compute is a query engine's problem rather than a description's. The two index conventions differ — XPath predicates are 1-based, JSON Pointer indices 0-based — and are kept as their standards define them, because normalising to one would surprise everyone who knows either.

XML paths need their prefixes bound with bddo:hasNamespaceBinding, and this is not optional in practice: PDS4, DIMAP and SAFE are all namespaced, and an unprefixed path against a namespaced document matches nothing rather than failing. The prefix is matched to the document's own namespace declarations rather than to whatever prefix the document happens to spell them with. An empty prefix binds the default namespace, which an abbreviated XPath cannot otherwise reach — an unprefixed step means "no namespace", not "the default one". A binding on a JSON document is rejected, since JSON Pointer has no prefixes and such a binding signals a misunderstanding of what the paths below will match.

A path selecting no node leaves the field absent; one selecting several is an error unless the field repeats, in which case its value is the selected nodes in document order. Every field of a tree document is located by exactly one bddo:nodePath and by nothing else — a byte offset or a record key here is a leftover from another container form and would address nothing.

Nested Records

A bddo:DelimitedRecords is flat unless it declares bddo:hasGrouping. That was enough for ESRI .hdr, ENVI and their kin, but stopped at the label formats that are otherwise the same shape — PVL/ODL, the ERMapper header, INI-style labels — for want of a closing token. Those labels always sat over a payload that was already a clean array; only the label could not be decomposed.

A bddo:RecordGrouping names one of three styles and the two tokens that delimit a group. Three styles rather than one general grammar, because each is fully described by two tokens plus where the group's NAME comes from — and a general nested-grammar surface would be a parser generator, which this is not.

bddo:groupingStyleOpens / closesGroup nameExample
bddo:keyedGroupA record whose key is the tokenThe opening record's valueOBJECT = IMAGE … END_OBJECT (PVL/ODL)
bddo:valuedGroupA record whose value is the tokenThe opening record's keyCoordinateSpace Begin … CoordinateSpace End (ERMapper)
bddo:sectionHeadedA record bracketed by the two tokens; the next one implicitly closes itThe text between the tokens[OVERVIEW] (INI)

Both tokens are always required. A grouping that says where a group starts but not where it ends cannot be read at all — every record after the first group would be inside it. bddo:sectionHeaded nests one deep: a section is ended by the next one, so sections cannot contain sections. That is what the style permits, not a limitation of this model.

Inside a grouped container a field is located by bddo:keyPath — the group names and finally the key, joined by bddo:pathSeparator (default /, declared on the container because a format whose group names contain a slash must choose something else). bddo:keyPath and bddo:key are mutually exclusive: a field cannot be found two ways, and a description asserting both would leave a reader to guess which wins. A path in a container that does not nest is rejected by bddo:KeyPathShape, because it would otherwise silently match nothing rather than fail.

Variable-Length Integers

An integer whose WIDTH is discovered from its own bytes rather than declared. Framing such a field was always possible byte by byte; reconstructing the VALUE was not, which is what kept protobuf-framed and SQLite-backed formats out of reach.

IRIEncodingxsdType
bddo:varuintUnsigned base-128 (LEB128): seven value bits per byte, little-endian, high bit set on every byte but the last. Protobuf's plain varint.xsd:nonNegativeInteger
bddo:varintLEB128 carrying a zigzag-encoded value, so small negatives stay short. Protobuf's sint32/sint64. Distinct from reading a varuint and reinterpreting the bits, which sign-extends instead.xsd:integer
bddo:sqliteVarintOne to nine BIG-endian bytes, high bit as continuation for the first eight, the ninth contributing all eight bits. A different scheme from LEB128, not a dialect of it; used by GeoPackage, MBTiles and Rasterlite.xsd:integer

A field of one of these types MUST NOT declare a width or terminator — bddo:size, bddo:sizeFromField, bddo:sizeFromExpression, bddo:sizeToEndOfStream, bddo:terminator or bddo:bitLength — because its length comes from the data and a declared one could contradict it. bddo:VarintWidthShape enforces this.

ASCII-Coded Numeric Types

Many record-oriented formats write numbers as text in fixed-width fields — NITF BCS-N, FITS card values, USGS DEM Fortran fields. bddo:asciiInteger and bddo:asciiDecimal read such a field as a number rather than a string, so its value may drive bddo:sizeFromExpression, bddo:atOffsetFromExpression and bddo:repeatCountFromExpression. The field's width comes from bddo:size (or a terminator), except for a field of a bddo:DelimitedRecords, whose extent comes from its container's delimiters; bddo:numericBase selects the radix.

Checksum Algorithms

A bddo:Checksum names its algorithm with bddo:checksumAlgorithm. The digests (bddo:md5, bddo:sha1, bddo:sha256) and bddo:adler32 are each one algorithm. CRCs are a family: every CRC in use is fixed by the six parameters of the Rocksoft model — width, polynomial, initial register value, input reflection, output reflection and final XOR — and two CRCs of one width and polynomial that differ in any other parameter give different values. BDDO therefore names the common variants and describes any other by its parameters.

IRICatalogue nameWidthPolynomialInitRefIn / RefOutXorOutCheck
bddo:crc8SmbusCRC-8/SMBUS80x070x00false / false0x000xF4
bddo:crc16CRC-16/IBM-3740 (CCITT-FALSE)160x10210xFFFFfalse / false0x00000x29B1
bddo:crc16ArcCRC-16/ARC160x80050x0000true / true0x00000xBB3D
bddo:crc16XmodemCRC-16/XMODEM160x10210x0000false / false0x00000x31C3
bddo:crc16ModbusCRC-16/MODBUS160x80050xFFFFtrue / true0x00000x4B37
bddo:crc16X25CRC-16/IBM-SDLC (X-25)160x10210xFFFFtrue / true0xFFFF0x906E
bddo:crc32CRC-32/ISO-HDLC320x04C11DB70xFFFFFFFFtrue / true0xFFFFFFFF0xCBF43926
bddo:crc32cCRC-32/ISCSI (CRC-32C)320x1EDC6F410xFFFFFFFFtrue / true0xFFFFFFFF0xE3069283
bddo:crc32Bzip2CRC-32/BZIP2320x04C11DB70xFFFFFFFFfalse / false0xFFFFFFFF0xFC891918
bddo:crc32Mpeg2CRC-32/MPEG-2320x04C11DB70xFFFFFFFFfalse / false0x000000000x0376E6E7
bddo:crc64Ecma182CRC-64/ECMA-182640x42F0E1EBA9EA36930x0000000000000000false / false0x00000000000000000x6C40DF5F0B497347
bddo:crc64XzCRC-64/XZ640x42F0E1EBA9EA36930xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFtrue / true0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF0x995DC9BBDF1939FA

Parameters and check values are those of the CRC catalogue (Greg Cook, Catalogue of parametrised CRC algorithms); the check value is the CRC of the nine ASCII bytes 123456789. Each named individual carries the same parameters as bddo:crcWidth, bddo:crcPolynomial, bddo:crcInit, bddo:crcReflectIn, bddo:crcReflectOut and bddo:crcXorOut, and its check value as bddo:crcCheck, so a name and its meaning travel together; a gate of this specification recomputes every check value from the parameters.

Any other CRC is a bddo:CustomCrc, usually a blank node in the bddo:checksumAlgorithm position, stating the six parameters. A bddo:CustomCrc MUST state each of the six exactly once: a width from 8 to 64, a polynomial (in normal, unreflected form, without its top term), an initial value and a final XOR that are non-negative integers below 2width, and the two reflection flags as xsd:boolean literals. bddo:CustomCrcShape enforces this. The computation is the Rocksoft model's: the register starts at the initial value; each input byte, bit-reversed first when bddo:crcReflectIn is true, is XORed into the top eight bits of the register, which is then shifted left once per bit, the polynomial being XORed in after each shift that carries a 1 out of the top bit; after the last byte the register is bit-reversed over its width when bddo:crcReflectOut is true, and XORed with the final XOR value.

ex:crc a bddo:Field ; bddo:dataType bddo:uint16le ;
    bddo:checksum [ a bddo:Checksum ; bddo:coversFromField ex:header ; bddo:coversToField ex:payload ;
        bddo:checksumAlgorithm [ a bddo:CustomCrc ; bddo:crcWidth 16 ; bddo:crcPolynomial 32773 ;  # 0x8005
            bddo:crcInit 0 ; bddo:crcReflectIn true ; bddo:crcReflectOut true ; bddo:crcXorOut 0 ] ] .

The value a CRC is compared with is the checksum field's own value, read with the field's declared data type, width and byte order, and that width is the CRC's: a CRC-16 stored little-endian is described by a bddo:uint16le field (or a bddo:uint16 in a little-endian struct), never by a property of the algorithm. The comparison is numeric (Processing Model, Checksum).

Parameterised Structs and Local Bindings

Two constructs let a struct compute with values it does not read from its own bytes.

Parameters. A struct MAY declare bddo:hasParameter, an ordered list of bddo:Parameter resources. A place that names such a struct as a type — a Field's bddo:dataType, a bddo:DataTypeRule's bddo:ruleDataType, a bddo:DispatchArm's bddo:armDataType — supplies the values with bddo:hasArgument, an ordered list of HEL expression strings, on that same Field, rule or arm. The arguments are positional: the n-th argument is the value of the n-th parameter. They are evaluated in the caller's context, with the struct that contains the field as instance, when the field is read, and inside the called struct each parameter is read as param.name. A parameter's name is the simple key of its IRI, as a field's is. bddo:parameterType, when present, restricts the value to one HEL value type: bddo:IntegerParameter, bddo:FloatParameter (an Integer argument is converted to Float), bddo:StringParameter, bddo:BytesParameter or bddo:BooleanParameter. A bddo:dispatchDefault has nowhere to state arguments, so it never names a parameterised struct.

ex:Table a bddo:Struct ; bddo:hasField ( ex:Table.count ex:Table.width ex:Table.rows ) .
ex:Table.count a bddo:Field ; bddo:dataType bddo:uint8 .
ex:Table.width a bddo:Field ; bddo:dataType bddo:uint8 .
ex:Table.rows  a bddo:Field ; bddo:dataType ex:Row ; bddo:hasArgument ( "instance.count" "instance.width" ) .

ex:Row a bddo:Struct ; bddo:hasParameter ( ex:Row.n ex:Row.w ) ; bddo:hasField ( ex:Row.cells ) .
ex:Row.n a bddo:Parameter ; bddo:parameterType bddo:IntegerParameter .
ex:Row.w a bddo:Parameter ; bddo:parameterType bddo:IntegerParameter .
ex:Row.cells a bddo:Field ; bddo:dataType bddo:bytes ; bddo:sizeFromExpression "param.w" ;
    bddo:repeatCountFromExpression "param.n" .

Every place that names a parameterised struct as a type MUST supply exactly one argument per parameter, and a place that names a struct without parameters, or a bddo:DataType, MUST supply none; bddo:ArgumentArityShape checks the three direct forms. Within one struct, a parameter MUST NOT share its name with a field, a local binding or another parameter of that struct. A struct MAY pass arguments to itself, directly or through other structs: the recursion ends when the data ends it, and the struct nesting depth limit (Processing Model, Resource Limits) bounds it otherwise. Parameters consume no bytes, are not fields of the parsed tree, and are never emitted.

Local bindings. A bddo:LocalBinding is a member of a struct's bddo:hasField list that is not a field: a name for the value of its bddo:localExpression, evaluated with the struct as instance when its position in the list is reached. Members after it, and the struct's own size expression, read it by bare name or instance.name; it consumes no bytes, is not part of the parsed tree and is never emitted. That is the whole difference from a derived field (bddo:valueFromExpression): a derived field is a field, visible in the tree and liftable, while a binding is a private intermediate. A local binding MUST be named by an IRI, MUST NOT share its name with a field, a parameter or another binding of its struct, and MUST NOT be a member of a text container (a delimited, key/value or tree container), whose members have no evaluation order; bddo:LocalBindingShape checks the first and the last. A text container — a bddo:DelimitedTable, bddo:DelimitedRecords, bddo:KeyValueHeader or bddo:TreeDocument — MUST NOT declare bddo:hasParameter: its members are located by key, column or path rather than read in order, so it has no scope to bind arguments in, and a processor raises a Description error for it (Processing Model, Errors).

ex:Image a bddo:Struct ; bddo:hasField ( ex:Image.width ex:Image.height ex:Image.area ex:Image.pixels ) .
ex:Image.width  a bddo:Field ; bddo:dataType bddo:uint16 .
ex:Image.height a bddo:Field ; bddo:dataType bddo:uint16 .
ex:Image.area   a bddo:LocalBinding ; bddo:localExpression "instance.width * instance.height" .
ex:Image.pixels a bddo:Field ; bddo:dataType bddo:bytes ; bddo:sizeFromExpression "instance.area" .

Properties

Structural Properties

bddo:hasField
Links a bddo:Struct to its ordered components. The object MUST be an rdf:List whose members are bddo:Field or bddo:LocalBinding resources, to preserve order.
bddo:hasParameter / bddo:parameterType
The ordered rdf:List of a parameterised struct's bddo:Parameter resources, and the optional kind of value one of them accepts (Parameterised Structs and Local Bindings).
bddo:localExpression
The HEL expression of a bddo:LocalBinding.
bddo:endianness
The byte order for a bddo:Struct, bddo:Field, or bddo:DataType. Value MUST be bddo:BigEndian or bddo:LittleEndian. The nearest declaration wins (DataType > Field > Struct > inherited from the parent).
bddo:hasConditionalEndianness
Ordered list of bddo:EndiannessRule; the first rule whose bddo:condition holds sets the byte order for this term and its descendants. For formats that declare their own byte order. Mutually exclusive with bddo:endianness.
bddo:bitOrder
The bit-packing order for bit-level fields in a Struct. Value MUST be bddo:MSBFirst or bddo:LSBFirst.
bddo:syncOnMarker
A byte sequence a processor MUST scan for before parsing this bddo:Struct. Value MUST be an xsd:hexBinary literal.
bddo:size / bddo:sizeFromField / bddo:sizeFromExpression (on a Struct)
Bounds the Struct's total byte extent, establishing a bounded region for its children: descendant reads MUST NOT cross it, bddo:sizeToEndOfStream resolves to the region end, and trailing/padding bytes are skipped. Essential for length-delimited containers (MP4/ISOBMFF boxes, EBML, chunk payloads). At most one mechanism.

Field Properties

bddo:dataType
The Field's default data type. Value MUST be a bddo:DataType or a bddo:Struct. A Field MUST have either a bddo:dataType or a bddo:hasConditionalDataType (or both).
bddo:hasConditionalDataType
An rdf:List of bddo:DataTypeRule allowing the processor to select a more specific data type at runtime.
bddo:hasDispatchTable
A bddo:DispatchTable selecting the Field's type by key: the parsed value of bddo:dispatchOnField (or the value of bddo:dispatchOnExpression) is compared by equality with the bddo:armKey of every bddo:DispatchArm whose bddo:armTable is this table; the matching arm's bddo:armDataType is the effective type, else bddo:dispatchDefault, else the Field's bddo:dataType. Arms are independent subjects, not list members, so a description that owl:imports another may add arms to its tables — the mechanism for open type registries (container box types, tagged extensions). Keys MUST be unique per table across all contributing graphs. Mutually exclusive with bddo:hasConditionalDataType, which remains the form for ordered, possibly overlapping predicate rules.
bddo:seekScope
On an offset-addressed Field: bddo:regionScope (default) confines the read to the innermost bounded region; bddo:streamScope resolves the target against the whole stream and suspends the region bound for that one read. Needed when a table inside one length-delimited container locates payloads stored in another.
bddo:size / bddo:sizeFromField / bddo:sizeFromExpression / bddo:sizeToEndOfStream
The size of the field in bytes: fixed, read from another field, computed by a HEL expression, or "consume the remainder of the stream" (xsd:boolean). A Field MUST use at most one sizing mechanism, and variable-length types (bddo:bytes, bddo:string) MUST use exactly one sizing or termination mechanism — except for a field of a bddo:DelimitedRecords, which is exempt because its extent is set by its container's delimiters.
bddo:terminator
A byte sequence terminating a variable-length field. Value MUST be an xsd:hexBinary literal.
bddo:trimNull
For a fixed-size (padded) bddo:string, the value is the bytes before the first NUL code unit of the field's encoding within its width, decoded; the NUL and the bytes after it are padding, neither decoded nor validated (NUL-padded names, e.g. TAR/ZIP; Processing Model, Decoding text).
bddo:encoding
The character encoding of a bddo:string field. Value MUST be one of the encoding individuals (bddo:utf8, bddo:ascii, bddo:utf16le, bddo:utf16be, bddo:latin1). Defaults to bddo:utf8.
bddo:numericBase
Radix (8, 10 or 16) for reading a bddo:asciiInteger field. Default 10.
bddo:bitLength
The width, in bits, of a sub-byte field (see Bit-Level Fields).
bddo:repeatCount / bddo:repeatCountFromField / bddo:repeatCountFromExpression / bddo:repeatUntil
Turn the field into an array parsed a fixed number of times, a number read from another field, a number computed by a HEL expression, or repeatedly until a HEL boolean expression evaluates to true. A Field MUST use at most one repetition mechanism.
bddo:atOffset / bddo:atOffsetFromField / bddo:atOffsetFromExpression / bddo:offsetBase / bddo:alignment
Addressing and alignment (see Sequencing, Addressing & Alignment).
bddo:hasFixedValue
A constant expected value, used to validate markers and magic numbers. The literal is compared against the parsed value after decoding according to the field's data type.
bddo:isPresentIf
A HEL boolean expression; the field is present only if it evaluates to true.
bddo:valueFromExpression
Declares a derived, zero-byte field: its value is computed by a HEL expression rather than read from the stream. The field consumes no bytes; its computed value is placed in the parse context under the field's name so later sizing, offset, presence, mapping and validation expressions may reference it. Such a field declares no bddo:dataType and no sizing, offset or bit-length; the result's type is that of the HEL expression.
bddo:validIf
A HEL boolean expression that MUST hold once this field is parsed; if it evaluates to false the parse fails with a validation error. Use for post-parse constraints such as a bound on a count. Richer, graph-level constraints belong in SHACL at the semantic layer.
bddo:enumeration
Links a Field to a bddo:Enumeration that maps its raw value to a named symbol. If the Enumeration sets bddo:enumIsFlags true, the raw value is a bitmask and every matching flag symbol is emitted.
bddo:checksum
Links a Field (the one storing the checksum value) to a bddo:Checksum describing the algorithm and covered range.
bddo:hasArgument
The ordered rdf:List of HEL expression strings that supply the parameters of the parameterised struct a Field, a bddo:DataTypeRule or a bddo:DispatchArm names as its type (Parameterised Structs and Local Bindings).
bddo:recordDelimiter / bddo:fieldDelimiter / bddo:keyValueSeparator
On a bddo:DelimitedRecords (see Delimited Text Records): the byte sequence splitting the byte run into records, splitting a record into values, and (in a bddo:KeyValueHeader) splitting a key from its value. Each value MUST be an xsd:hexBinary literal.
bddo:quoteChar / bddo:escapeChar
On a bddo:DelimitedRecords: the byte that quotes a value so it may contain a delimiter, and the byte that escapes the following byte. Values MUST be xsd:hexBinary literals.
bddo:commentPrefix / bddo:skipRecords / bddo:trimWhitespace
On a bddo:DelimitedRecords: a leading string marking a record as a comment to ignore, a count of leading records to discard before parsing, and whether to strip leading/trailing whitespace from every key and value.
bddo:key / bddo:keyIsCaseInsensitive
On a Field of a bddo:KeyValueHeader: the key naming the field, which replaces positional location, and whether key matching ignores ASCII case.

Data Type, Rule, Enumeration & Checksum Properties

bddo:baseType
The fundamental kind of data. Value MUST be one of bddo:baseInteger, bddo:baseFloat, bddo:baseString, or bddo:baseBytes.
bddo:bitWidth, bddo:isSigned, bddo:xsdType
Describe a bddo:DataType: its width in bits, its signedness, and the RDF literal datatype produced on output.
bddo:condition, bddo:ruleDataType
A bddo:DataTypeRule's HEL condition and the data type (a bddo:DataType or bddo:Struct) to apply when it holds.
bddo:condition, bddo:ruleEndianness
A bddo:EndiannessRule's HEL condition and the byte order (bddo:BigEndian or bddo:LittleEndian) to apply when it holds.
bddo:hasEnumValue, bddo:enumRawValue, bddo:enumSymbol
An bddo:Enumeration's members; each bddo:EnumValue pairs a raw literal (bddo:enumRawValue) with an optional symbolic IRI (bddo:enumSymbol) and/or rdfs:label.
bddo:checksumAlgorithm, bddo:coversFromField, bddo:coversToField, bddo:coversFromExpression, bddo:coversToExpression
A bddo:Checksum's algorithm and the byte range it protects. The algorithm is one of the named algorithms of Checksum Algorithms or a bddo:CustomCrc — a closed set of names plus one parameterised form, since a checksum names an algorithm a reader must already implement to verify anything, and every CRC is one algorithm with six parameters. Each end of the range is stated independently, as a field reference or a HEL expression, so a range may start at a named field and end at a computed offset. The field form is inclusive of coversToField's last byte; the expression form is the half-open [start,end). (bddo:coversExpression, a single expression for the whole range, is deprecated in favour of the pair.)

Constraint language follows W3C SHACL; publication and persistent term identifiers follow the W3C vocabulary publication guidance. Concept definitions and scope notes use SKOS documentation properties.

Node shape

bddo:ArgumentArityShape — Argument Arity Shape

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#ArgumentArityShape

Label
Argument Arity Shape
Definition
Wherever a bddo:Struct that declares parameters is named as a type (by bddo:dataType, bddo:ruleDataType or bddo:armDataType), the naming node supplies exactly one bddo:hasArgument list member per parameter. List nodes are counted, so two equal argument strings count twice.
Declared RDF type
sh:NodeShape
Domain / range
Not applicable to this node shape. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Activation
subjects using bddo:armDataType, bddo:dataType, bddo:ruleDataType
Severity
SHACL default: sh:Violation. This applies to results of this shape; nested property shapes have their own severity (default sh:Violation), rather than inheriting it. Rules generate triples instead of validation results.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Constraint and rule specification
  • rdf:type: sh:NodeShape
  • SPARQL validation constraint:
    • Validation message: "{?s} declares {?np} parameter(s), but {?na} argument(s) are supplied for it."
    • SPARQL prefix declarations: bddo:_prefixes
    • SELECT query returning violations: 
      SELECT $this ?s (COUNT(DISTINCT ?pn) AS ?np) (COUNT(DISTINCT ?an) AS ?na) WHERE {
            $this ?tp ?s .
            FILTER(?tp IN (bddo:dataType, bddo:ruleDataType, bddo:armDataType))
            ?s a bddo:Struct .
            OPTIONAL { ?s bddo:hasParameter/rdf:rest* ?pn . FILTER(?pn != rdf:nil) }
            OPTIONAL { $this bddo:hasArgument/rdf:rest* ?an . FILTER(?an != rdf:nil) }
        } GROUP BY $this ?s HAVING (COUNT(DISTINCT ?pn) != COUNT(DISTINCT ?an))
  • Target subjects of: bddo:armDataType
  • Target subjects of: bddo:dataType
  • Target subjects of: bddo:ruleDataType

Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Constraint and rule specification
  • rdf:type: sh:NodeShape
  • SPARQL validation constraint:
    • Validation message: "A bddo:asciiInteger / bddo:asciiDecimal field must declare a width (size / sizeFromField / sizeFromExpression), a terminator, or bddo:separatedBy."
    • SPARQL prefix declarations: bddo:_prefixes
    • SELECT query returning violations: 
      SELECT $this WHERE {
            $this bddo:dataType ?dt .
            FILTER(?dt IN (bddo:asciiInteger, bddo:asciiDecimal))
            FILTER NOT EXISTS { $this bddo:size ?s }
            FILTER NOT EXISTS { $this bddo:sizeFromField ?f }
            FILTER NOT EXISTS { $this bddo:sizeFromExpression ?e }
            FILTER NOT EXISTS { $this bddo:terminator ?t }
            FILTER NOT EXISTS { $this bddo:separatedBy ?sep }
            FILTER NOT EXISTS {
                ?p bddo:hasField/rdf:rest*/rdf:first $this .
                ?p a ?c .
                # Same exemption as bddo:VariableLengthFieldShape, and it must be kept in step
                # with it: both ask "does the container decide this field's extent?", and a new
                # container form has to be listed in both or a description conforms to one shape
                # and not the other.
                FILTER(?c IN (bddo:DelimitedRecords, bddo:KeyValueHeader, bddo:DelimitedTable,
                              bddo:TreeDocument))
            }
        }
  • Target class: bddo:Field

Class

bddo:Checksum — Checksum

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#Checksum

Label
Checksum
Definition
A checksum/hash over a byte range. Each END of the range is stated independently: a field reference (coversFromField/coversToField) or a HEL expression yielding a byte offset (coversFromExpression/coversToExpression). The two ends may use different mechanisms -- a checksum covering everything from a named header field to a length carried elsewhere needs exactly that. Note the boundary convention differs by mechanism, because each follows what its inputs naturally give: the field form runs from the FIRST byte of coversFromField to the LAST byte of coversToField (inclusive), while the expression form is the half-open [start,end) that the two offsets delimit.
Declared RDF type
owl:Class
Domain / range
Not applicable to this class. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Shape usage / validation context
bddo:ChecksumShape, bddo:FieldShape
Cardinality
Context-dependent. The linked shapes state minimum/maximum counts at their value paths; no universal cardinality is inferred.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Declared axioms and values
  • rdf:type: owl:Class

bddo:ChecksumShape — Checksum Shape

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#ChecksumShape

Label
Checksum Shape
Definition
Applies to instances of bddo:Checksum; objects of bddo:checksum. bddo:checksumAlgorithm is one of the named algorithms or a bddo:CustomCrc. bddo:coversExpression takes at most one value; each is a HEL expression (an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal). bddo:coversFromExpression takes at most one value; each is a HEL expression (an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal). bddo:coversFromField takes at most one value; each is an instance of bddo:Field. bddo:coversToExpression takes at most one value; each is a HEL expression (an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal). bddo:coversToField takes at most one value; each is an instance of bddo:Field.
Declared RDF type
sh:NodeShape
Domain / range
Not applicable to this node shape. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Activation
instances of bddo:Checksum; objects of bddo:checksum
Severity
SHACL default: sh:Violation. This applies to results of this shape; nested property shapes have their own severity (default sh:Violation), rather than inheriting it. Rules generate triples instead of validation results.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Constraint and rule specification
  • rdf:type: sh:NodeShape
  • Must NOT conform to:
    • Every branch must conform (AND):
        • Property constraint:
          • Minimum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
          • Value path: bddo:coversExpression
        • At least one alternative must conform (OR):
            • Property constraint:
              • Minimum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>

bddo:CustomCrc — Custom CRC

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#CustomCrc

Label
Custom CRC
Definition
A CRC stated by its Rocksoft-model parameters rather than named: bddo:crcWidth (8..64 bits), bddo:crcPolynomial, bddo:crcInit, bddo:crcReflectIn, bddo:crcReflectOut and bddo:crcXorOut, all six required. Used as the value of bddo:checksumAlgorithm for a CRC that no named algorithm identifies.
Declared RDF type
owl:Class

bddo:CustomCrcShape — Custom Crc Shape

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#CustomCrcShape

Label
Custom Crc Shape
Definition
Applies to instances of bddo:CustomCrc. A bddo:CustomCrc states exactly one bddo:crcInit. A bddo:CustomCrc states exactly one bddo:crcPolynomial. A bddo:CustomCrc states exactly one bddo:crcReflectIn. A bddo:CustomCrc states exactly one bddo:crcReflectOut. A bddo:CustomCrc states exactly one bddo:crcWidth, an integer from 8 to 64. A bddo:CustomCrc states exactly one bddo:crcXorOut.

  • Property constraint:
    • Maximum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Validation message: "A bddo:CustomCrc states exactly one bddo:crcInit."
    • Minimum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Value path: bddo:crcInit
  • Property constraint:
    • Maximum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Validation message: "A bddo:CustomCrc states exactly one bddo:crcPolynomial."
    • Minimum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Value path: bddo:crcPolynomial
  • Property constraint:
    • Maximum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Validation message: "A bddo:CustomCrc states exactly one bddo:crcReflectIn."
    • Minimum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Value path: bddo:crcReflectIn

    • Maximum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Validation message: "A bddo:CustomCrc states exactly one bddo:crcReflectOut."
    • Minimum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Value path: bddo:crcReflectOut
  • Property constraint:
    • Maximum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Validation message: "A bddo:CustomCrc states exactly one bddo:crcXorOut."
    • Minimum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Value path: bddo:crcXorOut
  • SPARQL validation constraint:
    • Validation message: "{?p} {?v} does not fit a {?w}-bit CRC: it must be below 2^{?w}."
    • SPARQL prefix declarations: bddo:_prefixes
    • SELECT query returning violations: 
      SELECT $this ?p ?v ?w WHERE {
            $this bddo:crcWidth ?w ; ?p ?v .
            FILTER(?p IN (bddo:crcPolynomial, bddo:crcInit, bddo:crcXorOut))
            FILTER(isNumeric(?w) && isNumeric(?v) && ?w >= 8 && ?w <= 64)
            BIND(FLOOR(?w / 8) AS ?k)
            BIND(?w - 8 * ?k AS ?r)
            BIND(IF(?k = 1, 256, IF(?k = 2, 65536, IF(?k = 3, 16777216, IF(?k = 4, 4294967296,
                 IF(?k = 5, 1099511627776, IF(?k = 6, 281474976710656, IF(?k = 7, 72057594037927936,
                 18446744073709551616))))))) AS ?bytes)
            BIND(IF(?r = 0, 1, IF(?r = 1, 2, IF(?r = 2, 4, IF(?r = 3, 8, IF(?r = 4, 16, IF(?r = 5, 32,
                 IF(?r = 6, 64, 128))))))) AS ?bits)
            FILTER(?v >= ?bytes * ?bits)
        }
  • Target class: bddo:CustomCrc

bddo:DispatchTable — Dispatch Table

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#DispatchTable

Label
Dispatch Table
Definition
A keyed, open selection of a field's effective type: the dispatch key is matched by equality against the keys of arms that any importing description may contribute.
Declared RDF type
owl:Class
Domain / range
Not applicable to this class. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Shape usage / validation context
bddo:DispatchArmShape, bddo:DispatchKeyUniquenessShape, bddo:DispatchTableShape, bddo:FieldShape
Cardinality
Context-dependent. The linked shapes state minimum/maximum counts at their value paths; no universal cardinality is inferred.
Additional source notes
A keyed, open selection of a Field's effective type. The value of the key (dispatchOnField or dispatchOnExpression) is matched by equality against the armKey of the table's DispatchArms; the matching arm's armDataType is the effective type, else dispatchDefault, else the Field's dataType. Arms are independent resources rather than list members, so a description that imports this one can contribute an arm without rewriting anything. Contrast hasConditionalDataType, whose ordered predicate rules are closed and may overlap.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Declared axioms and values
  • rdf:type: owl:Class

Node shape

bddo:FieldShape — Field Shape

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#FieldShape

Label
Field Shape
Definition
Applies to instances of bddo:Field. bddo:alignment takes at most one value; each is a number greater than 0. bddo:atOffset takes at most one value; each is a number at least 0. bddo:atOffsetFromExpression takes at most one value; each is a HEL expression (an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal). bddo:atOffsetFromField takes at most one value; each is an instance of bddo:Field. bddo:bitLength takes at most one value; each is a number greater than 0. bddo:checksum takes at most one value; each is an instance of bddo:Checksum. bddo:dataType takes at most one value; each is an IRI naming an instance of bddo:DataType or bddo:Struct. bddo:encoding takes at most one value; each is one of bddo:utf8, bddo:ascii, bddo:utf16le, bddo:utf16be or bddo:latin1. bddo:enumeration takes at most one value; each is an instance of bddo:Enumeration. bddo:hasDispatchTable takes at most one value; each is an instance of bddo:DispatchTable. bddo:isPresentIf takes at most one value; each is a HEL expression (an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal). bddo:offsetBase takes at most one value; each is one of bddo:streamStart, bddo:streamEnd, bddo:parentStart or bddo:currentPosition. bddo:rootKeyFromField takes at most one value; each is an instance of bddo:Field. bddo:seekScope takes at most one value; each is either bddo:regionScope or bddo:streamScope. bddo:separatedBy takes at most one value; each is an xsd:string literal equal to "whitespace". bddo:size takes at most one value; each is a number greater than 0. bddo:sizeFromExpression takes at most one value; each is a HEL expression (an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal). bddo:sizeFromField takes at most one value; each is an instance of bddo:Field. bddo:sizeToEndOfStream takes at most one value; each is an xsd:boolean literal. bddo:terminator takes at most one value; each is an xsd:hexBinary literal. bddo:trimNull takes at most one value; each is an xsd:boolean literal. bddo:validIf takes at most one value; each is a HEL expression (an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal). bddo:valueFromExpression takes at most one value; each is a HEL expression (an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal). Every member of a hasConditionalDataType list must be a bddo:DataTypeRule.
Declared RDF type
sh:NodeShape
Domain / range

            $this ?p ?v .

Datatype

bddo:HelExpression — HEL expression

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#HelExpression

Label
HEL expression
Definition
The datatype of a literal whose text is a HEL expression. Its lexical space is the text of HEL expressions as the HEL specification defines it, and a literal's value is that text as a string: the value space is a subset of xsd:string's, so a bddo:HelExpression literal is a value of every property whose range is xsd:string. Authoring tools SHOULD type expressions with it; an expression typed xsd:string remains valid. The datatype does not check the text: whether it parses is for the HEL evaluator to say.
Declared RDF type
rdfs:Datatype
Domain / range
Not applicable to this datatype. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Superclass / class expression
xsd:string
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Shape usage / validation context
bddo:DataTypeRuleShape, bddo:EndiannessRuleShape, bddo:FieldShape, bddo:HelExpressionValueShape, bddo:StructShape, conf:ConstraintShape, conf:RangeDatatypeShape, hexplain:ClassMappingRuleShape, hexplain:MappingRuleShape, hexplain:RangeDatatypeShape, hexplain:ValueExpressionShape, dlv:CellDataTypeRuleShape, dlv:DimensionOrderRuleShape, dlv:DimensionShape, dlv:RangeDatatypeShape
Cardinality
Context-dependent. The linked shapes state minimum/maximum counts at their value paths; no universal cardinality is inferred.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Declared axioms and values
  • rdf:type: rdfs:Datatype
  • rdfs:subClassOf: xsd:string

bddo:KeyPathShape — Key Path Shape

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#KeyPathShape

Label
Key Path Shape
Definition
Applies to instances of bddo:Field. bddo:keyPath takes at most one value; each is an xsd:string literal. bddo:keyPath locates a field inside nested groups, so its container must declare bddo:hasGrouping; without one the container is flat and the path addresses nothing.
Declared RDF type
sh:NodeShape
Domain / range

bddo:KeyValueHeader — Key/Value Header

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#KeyValueHeader

Label
Key/Value Header
Definition
DelimitedRecords whose records are key/value pairs split by keyValueSeparator. Each expected key is a Field carrying bddo:key, or, with no bddo:key, named by its simple key (the local name of its IRI after the last '.'); record order is not significant.

Class

bddo:LocalBinding — Local Binding

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#LocalBinding

Label
Local Binding
Definition
A named HEL value local to one Struct instance: a member of the struct's bddo:hasField list whose bddo:localExpression is evaluated when its position in the list is reached, in the struct's context. Later members, and the struct's own size, count, offset and presence expressions evaluated after it, read it by bare name or instance.<name>. It consumes no bytes, is not part of the parsed tree and is never lifted. Its name is the simple key of its IRI, as for a Field.
Declared RDF type
owl:Class
Domain / range
Not applicable to this class. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Shape usage / validation context
bddo:LocalBindingShape, bddo:StructShape
Cardinality
Context-dependent. The linked shapes state minimum/maximum counts at their value paths; no universal cardinality is inferred.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Declared axioms and values
  • rdf:type: owl:Class

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#NodePathShape

Label
Node Path Shape
Definition
Applies to instances of bddo:Field; subjects using bddo:nodePath. bddo:nodePath takes at most one value; each is an xsd:string literal.
Declared RDF type
sh:NodeShape
Domain / range
Not applicable to this node shape. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Activation
instances of bddo:Field; subjects using bddo:nodePath
Severity
SHACL default: sh:Violation. This applies to results of this shape; nested property shapes have their own severity (default sh:Violation), rather than inheriting it. Rules generate triples instead of validation results.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Constraint and rule specification
  • rdf:type: sh:NodeShape
  • Property constraint:
    • Required literal datatype (exact SHACL datatype): xsd:string
    • Maximum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Validation message: "bddo:nodePath takes at most one value; each is an xsd:string literal."
    • Value path: bddo:nodePath
  • SPARQL validation constraint:
    • Validation message: "This bddo:nodePath ({?p}) is not a JSON Pointer (RFC 6901): it must be empty or a sequence of /-prefixed reference tokens, with ~ only in ~0 or ~1."
    • SPARQL prefix declarations: bddo:_prefixes
    • SELECT query returning violations: 
      SELECT $this ?p WHERE {
            $this bddo:nodePath ?p .
            ?container bddo:hasField/rdf:rest*/rdf:first $this ; bddo:treeSyntax bddo:json .
            FILTER(!REGEX(?p, "^(/([^~/]|~[01])*)*$"))
        }
  • SPARQL validation constraint:
    • Validation message: "This bddo:nodePath ({?p}) is not an abbreviated XPath of child steps, optional [n] positions (n >= 1) and at most one final @attribute step."
    • SPARQL prefix declarations: bddo:_prefixes
    • SELECT query returning violations: 
      SELECT $this ?p WHERE {
            $this bddo:nodePath ?p .
            ?container bddo:hasField/rdf:rest*/rdf:first $this ; bddo:treeSyntax bddo:xml .
            FILTER(!REGEX(?p, "^(/?([^/@\\[\\]:\\s]+:)?[^/@\\[\\]:\\s]+(\\[[1-9][0-9]*\\])?(/([^/@\\[\\]:\\s]+:)?[^/@\\[\\]:\\s]+(\\[[1-9][0-9]*\\])?)*(/@([^/@\\[\\]:\\s]+:)?[^/@\\[\\]:\\s]+)?|@([^/@\\[\\]:\\s]+:)?[^/@\\[\\]:\\s]+)$"))
        }
  • SPARQL validation constraint:
    • Validation message: "bddo:nodePath locates a node in a parsed tree, so every container of the field must be a bddo:TreeDocument ({?container} is not, or there is none)."
    • SPARQL prefix declarations: bddo:_prefixes
    • SELECT query returning violations: 
      SELECT $this ?container WHERE {
            $this bddo:nodePath ?p .
            {
                FILTER NOT EXISTS { ?any bddo:hasField/rdf:rest*/rdf:first $this }
            } UNION {
                ?container bddo:hasField/rdf:rest*/rdf:first $this .
                FILTER NOT EXISTS { ?container a bddo:TreeDocument }
            }
        }
  • Target class: bddo:Field
  • Target subjects of: bddo:nodePath

bddo:Parameter — Parameter

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#Parameter

Label
Parameter
Definition
One formal parameter of a parameterised Struct, a member of its bddo:hasParameter list. Its name is the simple key of its IRI (the local name after the last '.'), as for a Field; HEL reads the value the caller supplied as param.<name>. A parameter consumes no bytes, is not a field of the parsed tree and is never lifted.

Class

bddo:RecordGrouping — Record Grouping

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#RecordGrouping

Label
Record Grouping
Definition
How a DelimitedRecords container nests: which records open a group, which close it, and where the group's name comes from. A container without one is flat, which is what every DelimitedRecords was before nesting existed.
Declared RDF type
owl:Class
Domain / range
Not applicable to this class. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Shape usage / validation context
bddo:GroupedRecordsShape, bddo:RecordGroupingShape
Cardinality
Context-dependent. The linked shapes state minimum/maximum counts at their value paths; no universal cardinality is inferred.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Declared axioms and values
  • rdf:type: owl:Class
Node shape

bddo:RecordGroupingShape — Record Grouping Shape

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#RecordGroupingShape

Label
Record Grouping Shape
Definition
Applies to instances of bddo:RecordGrouping. bddo:groupCloseToken takes exactly one value, an xsd:string literal. bddo:groupOpenToken takes exactly one value, an xsd:string literal. A bddo:RecordGrouping must name exactly one style: keyedGroup, valuedGroup or sectionHeaded.
Declared RDF type
sh:NodeShape
Domain / range
Not applicable to this node shape. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Activation
instances of bddo:RecordGrouping
Severity
SHACL default: sh:Violation. This applies to results of this shape; nested property shapes have their own severity (default sh:Violation), rather than inheriting it. Rules generate triples instead of validation results.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Constraint and rule specification

  • Property constraint:
    • Required literal datatype (exact SHACL datatype): xsd:string
    • Maximum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Validation message: "bddo:groupCloseToken takes exactly one value, an xsd:string literal."
    • Minimum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Value path: bddo:groupCloseToken
  • Property constraint:

    • Maximum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>

    • Minimum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>

bddo:StructShape — Struct Shape

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#StructShape

Label
Struct Shape
Definition
Applies to instances of bddo:Struct. bddo:bitOrder takes at most one value; each is either bddo:MSBFirst or bddo:LSBFirst. bddo:endianness takes at most one value; each is either bddo:BigEndian or bddo:LittleEndian. bddo:hasField must point to a single rdf:List. bddo:size takes at most one value; each is a number greater than 0. bddo:sizeFromExpression takes at most one value; each is a HEL expression (an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal). bddo:sizeFromField takes at most one value; each is an instance of bddo:Field. bddo:syncOnMarker takes at most one value; each is an xsd:hexBinary literal. Every member of a Struct's hasField list must be a bddo:Field or a bddo:LocalBinding.
Declared RDF type
sh:NodeShape
Domain / range
Not applicable to this node shape. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Activation

Severity
SHACL default: sh:Violation. This applies to results of this shape; nested property shapes have their own severity (default sh:Violation), rather than inheriting it. Rules generate triples instead of validation results.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl

    • Required value class: bddo:Field

    • Validation message: "bddo:sizeFromField takes at most one value; each is an instance of bddo:Field."
    • Value path: bddo:sizeFromField
  • Property constraint:
    • Required literal datatype (exact SHACL datatype): xsd:hexBinary
    • Maximum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Validation message: "bddo:syncOnMarker takes at most one value; each is an xsd:hexBinary literal."
    • Value path: bddo:syncOnMarker
  • Property constraint:
    • Allowed values:
      1. bddo:BigEndian
      2. bddo:LittleEndian
    • Maximum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Validation message: "bddo:endianness takes at most one value; each is either bddo:BigEndian or bddo:LittleEndian."
    • Value path: bddo:endianness
  • Property constraint:
    • Allowed values:
      1. bddo:MSBFirst
      2. bddo:LSBFirst

bddo:TreeDocumentShape — Tree Document Shape

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#TreeDocumentShape

Label
Tree Document Shape
Definition
Applies to instances of bddo:TreeDocument. A bddo:TreeDocument must declare at least one field. Each value of bddo:hasNamespaceBinding is an instance of bddo:NamespaceBinding. A bddo:TreeDocument must declare exactly one bddo:treeSyntax: bddo:xml or bddo:json. Every field of a bddo:TreeDocument is located by exactly one bddo:nodePath, and not by a key, a key path or a byte offset. bddo:hasNamespaceBinding applies to an XML tree document; JSON Pointer has no namespace prefixes.

Node shape

bddo:TreePrefixBindingShape — Tree Prefix Binding Shape

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#TreePrefixBindingShape

Label
Tree Prefix Binding Shape
Definition
An XML tree document binds each namespace prefix at most once, and every prefix its fields' bddo:nodePath values use is bound by one of its bddo:hasNamespaceBinding entries; only xml is predeclared.
Declared RDF type
sh:NodeShape
Domain / range
Not applicable to this node shape. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Activation
instances of bddo:TreeDocument; subjects using bddo:hasNamespaceBinding
Severity
SHACL default: sh:Violation. This applies to results of this shape; nested property shapes have their own severity (default sh:Violation), rather than inheriting it. Rules generate triples instead of validation results.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Constraint and rule specification
  • rdf:type: sh:NodeShape
  • SPARQL validation constraint:
    • Validation message: "A bddo:nodePath of this XML tree document ({?p}) uses a prefix that no bddo:hasNamespaceBinding binds (only xml is predeclared)."
    • SPARQL prefix declarations: bddo:_prefixes
    • SELECT query returning violations: 
      SELECT $this ?p WHERE {
            { SELECT $this (GROUP_CONCAT(?px; separator="|") AS ?alts) WHERE {
                $this bddo:treeSyntax bddo:xml .
                OPTIONAL { $this bddo:hasNamespaceBinding/bddo:namespacePrefix ?px . FILTER(?px != "") }
            } GROUP BY $this }
            $this bddo:hasField/rdf:rest*/rdf:first ?f .
            ?f bddo:nodePath ?p .
            # Each bound prefix is matched literally: an NCName may hold '.' and '-', which a
            # regular expression reads as metacharacters, so each is put in a class of its own.
            BIND(REPLACE(?alts, "([.-])", "[$1]") AS ?quoted)
            BIND(REPLACE(?p, CONCAT("(^|[/@])(", IF(?quoted != "", CONCAT(?quoted, "|"), ""), "xml):"), "$1") AS ?rest)
            FILTER(REGEX(?rest, "(^|[/@])[^/@:]+:"))
        }
  • SPARQL validation constraint:
    • Validation message: "Two bddo:hasNamespaceBinding entries of one tree document bind the prefix '{?px}'; a prefix is bound at most once."
    • SPARQL prefix declarations: bddo:_prefixes
    • SELECT query returning violations: 
      SELECT $this ?px WHERE {
            $this bddo:hasNamespaceBinding ?a , ?b .
            ?a bddo:namespacePrefix ?px . ?b bddo:namespacePrefix ?px .
            FILTER(!sameTerm(?a, ?b))
        }
  • Target class: bddo:TreeDocument
  • Target subjects of: bddo:hasNamespaceBinding

bddo:VariableLengthFieldShape — Variable Length Field Shape

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#VariableLengthFieldShape

Label
Variable Length Field Shape
Definition
Applies to instances of bddo:Field. A bddo:bytes or bddo:string field must declare a sizing or termination mechanism.
Declared RDF type
sh:NodeShape
Domain / range
Not applicable to this node shape. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Activation
instances of bddo:Field
Severity
SHACL default: sh:Violation. This applies to results of this shape; nested property shapes have their own severity (default sh:Violation), rather than inheriting it. Rules generate triples instead of validation results.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Constraint and rule specification
  • rdf:type: sh:NodeShape
  • SPARQL validation constraint:
    • Validation message: "A bddo:bytes or bddo:string field must declare a sizing or termination mechanism."
    • SPARQL prefix declarations: bddo:_prefixes
    • SELECT query returning violations: 
      SELECT $this WHERE {
            $this bddo:dataType ?dt . FILTER(?dt IN (bddo:bytes, bddo:string))
            FILTER NOT EXISTS {
                $this ?p ?v .
                FILTER(?p IN (bddo:size, bddo:sizeFromField, bddo:sizeFromExpression,
                              bddo:sizeToEndOfStream, bddo:terminator))
            }
            FILTER NOT EXISTS {
                ?p bddo:hasField/rdf:rest*/rdf:first $this .
                ?p a ?c .
                # Every container form that determines a field's extent ITSELF -- a delimited
                # record ends at its delimiter, a tree node at its own boundary -- so a declared
                # width would be a second, contradictory answer. Enumerated rather than inferred
                # via rdfs:subClassOf, because these shapes run without inference; a new
                # container form has to be added here too.
                FILTER(?c IN (bddo:DelimitedRecords, bddo:KeyValueHeader, bddo:DelimitedTable,
                              bddo:TreeDocument))
            }
        }
  • Target class: bddo:Field

bddo:asciiDecimal — asciiDecimal

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#asciiDecimal

Label
asciiDecimal
Definition
A fixed- or floating-point number written as text, occupying the field's declared width or ending at its terminator. Accepts leading sign, decimal point and an exponent introduced by E, e, D or d -- Fortran writes a double-precision exponent with D, and formats written by Fortran (USGSDEM record A) use it. A value spelled 'nan' (any case, optional leading sign) reads as a not-a-number; an infinite value is not accepted. Yields a HEL Float.
Declared RDF type

bddo:condition — condition

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#condition

Label
condition
Definition
The HEL Boolean expression guarding a physical parsing rule (a bddo:DataTypeRule or bddo:EndiannessRule, or a dlv:CellDataTypeRule or dlv:DimensionOrderRule): evaluated while the bytes are read, in the context of the field or struct being parsed, it decides which datatype, byte order, cell type or dimension order applies. It never chooses the RDF a node is lifted to; that is hexplain:condition.

bddo:crc16 — CRC-16

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#crc16

Label
CRC-16
Definition
The CRC-16/CCITT-FALSE integrity check: polynomial 0x1021, initial value 0xFFFF, no input or output reflection and final XOR 0x0000 (check value 0x29B1 over the ASCII bytes 123456789). Other CRC-16 variants, such as CRC-16/ARC or CRC-16/XMODEM, have identifiers of their own.
Declared RDF type
owl:NamedIndividual, bddo:ChecksumAlgorithm
Domain / range
Not applicable to this named individual. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Shape usage / validation context
bddo:ChecksumShape
Cardinality
Context-dependent. The linked shapes state minimum/maximum counts at their value paths; no universal cardinality is inferred.
Additional source notes
CRC-16/CCITT-FALSE (the catalogue's CRC-16/IBM-3740): polynomial 0x1021, initial value 0xFFFF, no input or output reflection, final XOR 0x0000; check value 0x29B1 over the ASCII bytes "123456789". No other CRC-16 variant is identified by this term: CRC-16/ARC, XMODEM, MODBUS and X-25 have terms of their own, and any other is a bddo:CustomCrc. HDL: crc16.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Declared axioms and values
  • rdf:type: owl:NamedIndividual
  • rdf:type: bddo:ChecksumAlgorithm
  • bddo:crcCheck: "10673"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
  • bddo:crcInit: "65535"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
  • bddo:crcPolynomial: "4129"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
  • bddo:crcReflectIn: "false"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#boolean>
  • bddo:crcReflectOut: "false"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#boolean>
  • bddo:crcWidth: "16"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
  • bddo:crcXorOut: "0"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>

bddo:hasArgument — has argument

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#hasArgument

Label
has argument
Definition
Ordered rdf:List of HEL expression strings, the positional arguments for the parameterised Struct this resource names as a type: on a Field, for its bddo:dataType; on a DataTypeRule, for its bddo:ruleDataType; on a DispatchArm, for its bddo:armDataType. A bddo:dispatchDefault has nowhere to state arguments, so it never names a parameterised struct. Each is evaluated in the CALLER's context -- the struct containing the field -- at the point the field is read.

bddo:hasConditionalEndianness — has conditional endianness

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#hasConditionalEndianness

Label
has conditional endianness
Definition
Ordered rdf:List of EndiannessRule. The first rule whose condition holds selects the byte order for this Struct or Field and, as with bddo:endianness, for its descendants unless they override it. Use for formats that declare their own byte order, such as TIFF's 4949h/4D4Dh marker. Mutually exclusive with bddo:endianness.

Annotation property

bddo:helVersion — HEL version

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#helVersion

Label
HEL version
Definition
The HEL version, as major.minor ("1.0"), that the expressions of a format description are written against, stated at most once on the description's owl:Ontology. Optional: absent, it is the HEL version of the specification edition the description conforms to. An evaluator implementing an earlier minor version of the same major version can still evaluate every expression that calls no function it lacks; a description declaring a later version tells it why a function may be unknown.
Declared RDF type
owl:AnnotationProperty
RDFS domain
No global domain declared. SHACL usage scopes below do not create a global domain axiom.
RDFS range
xsd:string
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>

bddo:rootKeyFromField — root key from field

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#rootKeyFromField

Label
root key from field
Definition
After this Field is parsed, its value is also bound in the ROOT parsing context under the key given by the named sibling Field's parsed value, if that key is not already bound (first occurrence wins). Lets a later record address an earlier one through its discriminator -- `root.<key>.<field>` -- when the earlier record sits inside a repeated sequence that HEL cannot search. A neutral generalisation of the chunk-type aliasing that several chunked container formats need.

Datatype property

bddo:separatedBy — separated by

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#separatedBy

Label
separated by
Definition
The element extent of a field -- repeated or scalar -- whose value is a number written as text, separated rather than sized or terminated: each element is preceded and followed by a run of separator bytes, and the run belongs to the element that owns it so the byte sequence can be rebuilt exactly. The only defined value is "whitespace" (space, tab, CR, LF). A separator is a RUN and a terminator is a SEQUENCE: between two delimiters of a delimited table an empty value is meaningful, whereas an empty run is not a value at all. Permitted only on a field whose data type is a number written as text. Because the LAST element's trailing run consumes every separator byte up to the field's own extent (or end of stream), a field declaring bddo:separatedBy must either be the last field read from its enclosing region, or be immediately followed by data that does not itself begin with a separator byte (space, tab, CR, LF) -- otherwise the trailing run consumes bytes that belong to the following field.
Declared RDF type
owl:DatatypeProperty
RDFS domain
No global domain declared. SHACL usage scopes below do not create a global domain axiom.
RDFS range
xsd:string
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Shape usage / validation context
bddo:FieldShape, bddo:RangeDatatypeShape
Cardinality
Context-dependent. The linked shapes state minimum/maximum counts at their value paths; no universal cardinality is inferred.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Declared axioms and values
  • rdf:type: owl:DatatypeProperty
  • rdfs:range: xsd:string

bddo:sqliteVarint — sqliteVarint

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#sqliteVarint

Label
sqliteVarint
Definition
SQLite's variable-length integer: one to nine BIG-endian bytes, high bit as continuation for the first eight, the ninth contributing all eight bits. A different scheme from LEB128, not a dialect of it; used by SQLite-backed formats (GeoPackage, MBTiles, Rasterlite).

bddo:trimNull — trim null

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#trimNull

Label
trim null
Definition
For a fixed-size (padded) string field, the value is the bytes before the first NUL code unit of the field's encoding within its width (a 0x00 byte, or a 00 00 pair at an even offset in UTF-16), decoded; the NUL and the bytes after it are padding, neither decoded nor validated.

bddo:usesStruct — uses struct (deprecated)

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#usesStruct

Label
uses struct (deprecated)
Definition
Deprecated pre-release template reference: the subject Struct's effective field list is the named Struct's fields followed by its own. There is no single replacement term; new descriptions state the fields directly, or compose with a Field whose bddo:dataType is the other Struct (which nests rather than splices).

Definition
A HEL boolean expression that MUST hold once this field is parsed; if it evaluates false the parse fails with a validation error. Use for post-parse constraints such as a magic number or a bound on a count. Richer, graph-level constraints belong in SHACL at the semantic layer.

Datatype property

bddo:valueFromExpression — value from expression

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#valueFromExpression

Label
value from expression
Definition
A HEL expression giving the value of a derived, zero-byte field. The field consumes no bytes; its computed value is placed in the parse context under the field's name so later sizing, offset, presence, mapping and validation expressions may reference it. A field with bddo:valueFromExpression declares no bddo:dataType and no sizing, offset or bit-length; the result's type is that of the HEL expression.
Declared RDF type
owl:DatatypeProperty
RDFS domain
No global domain declared. SHACL usage scopes below do not create a global domain axiom.

xsd:string

Named individual

bddo:varint — varint

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#varint

Label
varint
Definition
Signed base-128 varint: LEB128 carrying a zigzag-encoded value, so small negatives stay short -- n maps to (n << 1) XOR (n >> 63). Protobuf's sint32/sint64. Distinct from reading an unsigned varint and reinterpreting the bits, which is protobuf's int32 and sign-extends instead.
Declared RDF type
owl:NamedIndividual, bddo:DataType

Datatype property

bddo:whitespaceSeparated — whitespace separated

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#whitespaceSeparated

Label
whitespace separated
Definition
If true, values are separated by a RUN of one or more whitespace bytes (space, tab, CR, LF) rather than by a fixed bddo:fieldDelimiter. A run of any length separates exactly two values, so consecutive whitespace never yields an empty value, and leading or trailing whitespace on a record is ignored. This is what column-aligned ASCII grids need -- 'ncols 100', or a row of numbers padded to line up -- which a fixed delimiter cannot express. Mutually exclusive with bddo:fieldDelimiter. When bddo:recordDelimiter is also absent the content is one flat stream of whitespace-separated values rather than rows, which is how an ASCII grid whose rows wrap across lines is read; the grid's shape then comes from the field's dlv:DataLayout or repeat count, not from line breaks.
Declared RDF type
owl:DatatypeProperty
RDFS domain
No global domain declared. SHACL usage scopes below do not create a global domain axiom.
RDFS range
xsd:boolean
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Shape usage / validation context
bddo:DelimitedRecordsShape, bddo:DelimitedTableShape, bddo:RangeDatatypeShape
Cardinality
Context-dependent. The linked shapes state minimum/maximum counts at their value paths; no universal cardinality is inferred.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Declared axioms and values
  • rdf:type: owl:DatatypeProperty
  • rdfs:range: xsd:boolean
Named individual

bddo:xml — XML

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#xml

Label
XML
Definition
Paths are ABBREVIATED XPATH: child steps, attribute steps (@name), and positional predicates ([n], 1-based). A deliberately small subset -- no axes, no functions, no comparison predicates -- because a description states where a value IS, and a path that can compute is a query engine's problem rather than a description's.
Declared RDF type
owl:NamedIndividual, bddo:TreeSyntax
Domain / range
Not applicable to this named individual. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
