Canonical vocabulary and shapes

# Binary Data Description Ontology (BDDO) 1.3 — vocabulary + SHACL shapes
# Canonical machine-readable artifact. Namespace: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#
# Modeling: properties declare rdfs:range (value type). Domain and cardinality
# are expressed in SHACL only (no rdfs:domain), to avoid over-committing subjects.
@prefix :       <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#> .
@prefix bddo:   <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#> .
@prefix owl:    <http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#> .
@prefix rdf:    <http://www.w3.org/1999/02/22-rdf-syntax-ns#> .
@prefix rdfs:   <http://www.w3.org/2000/01/rdf-schema#> .
@prefix xsd:    <http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#> .
@prefix sh:     <http://www.w3.org/ns/shacl#> .
@prefix dcterms:<http://purl.org/dc/terms/> .
@prefix skos:    <http://www.w3.org/2004/02/skos/core#> .
@prefix vann:   <http://purl.org/vocab/vann/> .

<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> a owl:Ontology ;
    owl:versionIRI <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo/1.3> ;
    owl:versionInfo "1.3" ;
    owl:priorVersion <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo/1.2> ;
    dcterms:modified "2026-09-27"^^xsd:date ;
    rdfs:label "Binary Data Description Ontology (BDDO)"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "Vocabulary for declaratively describing the physical structure of binary data."@en ;
    dcterms:created "2025-08-16"^^xsd:date ;
    dcterms:creator <https://geoknoesis.com> ;
    dcterms:license <https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/> ;
    vann:preferredNamespacePrefix "bddo" ;
    vann:preferredNamespaceUri "https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#" .

# ---------- Classes ----------
:Struct       a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Struct"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "An ordered sequence of fields; a container in a binary format."@en .
:Field        a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Field"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "A single element within a Struct: a value, a bit slice, or an array."@en .
:DataType     a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Data Type"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
:DataTypeRule a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Data Type Rule"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
:Enumeration  a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Enumeration"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
:EnumValue    a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Enumeration Value"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
:Checksum     a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Checksum"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "A checksum/hash over a byte range. Each END of the range is stated independently: a field reference (coversFromField/coversToField) or a HEL expression yielding a byte offset (coversFromExpression/coversToExpression). The two ends may use different mechanisms -- a checksum covering everything from a named header field to a length carried elsewhere needs exactly that. Note the boundary convention differs by mechanism, because each follows what its inputs naturally give: the field form runs from the FIRST byte of coversFromField to the LAST byte of coversToField (inclusive), while the expression form is the half-open [start,end) that the two offsets delimit."@en .
:EndiannessRule a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Endianness Rule"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "One arm of a hasConditionalEndianness list: a HEL condition and the byte order it selects."@en .
:DispatchTable a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Dispatch Table"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "A keyed, open selection of a Field's effective type. The value of the key (dispatchOnField or dispatchOnExpression) is matched by equality against the armKey of the table's DispatchArms; the matching arm's armDataType is the effective type, else dispatchDefault, else the Field's dataType. Arms are independent resources rather than list members, so a description that imports this one can contribute an arm without rewriting anything. Contrast hasConditionalDataType, whose ordered predicate rules are closed and may overlap."@en .
:DispatchArm a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Dispatch Arm"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "One key-to-type association of a DispatchTable: the type a field takes when the table's key equals armKey. Keys are unique within a table across every graph that contributes to it."@en .
:SeekScope a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Seek Scope"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "How far an offset-addressed read may reach: the innermost bounded region (the default, regionScope) or the whole stream (streamScope)."@en .
# A struct that is instantiated with values: the same row layout used for several widths, or a
# record whose element count is carried by the struct that holds it. Parameters are named like
# fields (by the simple key of their IRI) and read in HEL through the root `param`.
:Parameter a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Parameter"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "One formal parameter of a parameterised Struct, a member of its bddo:hasParameter list. Its name is the simple key of its IRI (the local name after the last '.'), as for a Field; HEL reads the value the caller supplied as param.<name>. A parameter consumes no bytes, is not a field of the parsed tree and is never lifted."@en .
# A named intermediate value, computed once where it stands in the member list. It is a member
# of hasField (so its position, and therefore what it may read and what may read it, is the
# list order) but not a field: it consumes no bytes and is neither in the parsed tree nor lifted.
:ParameterType a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Parameter Type"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The kind of HEL value a Parameter accepts: one of the five individuals below, one per HEL scalar value type."@en .
:IntegerParameter a owl:NamedIndividual, :ParameterType ; rdfs:label "Integer parameter"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The parameter accepts a HEL Integer."@en .
:FloatParameter a owl:NamedIndividual, :ParameterType ; rdfs:label "Float parameter"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The parameter accepts a HEL Float; an Integer argument is converted to the Float of the same value."@en .
:StringParameter a owl:NamedIndividual, :ParameterType ; rdfs:label "String parameter"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The parameter accepts a HEL String."@en .
:BytesParameter a owl:NamedIndividual, :ParameterType ; rdfs:label "Bytes parameter"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The parameter accepts a HEL Bytes value."@en .
:BooleanParameter a owl:NamedIndividual, :ParameterType ; rdfs:label "Boolean parameter"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The parameter accepts a HEL Boolean."@en .
:LocalBinding a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Local Binding"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "A named HEL value local to one Struct instance: a member of the struct's bddo:hasField list whose bddo:localExpression is evaluated when its position in the list is reached, in the struct's context. Later members, and the struct's own size, count, offset and presence expressions evaluated after it, read it by bare name or instance.<name>. It consumes no bytes, is not part of the parsed tree and is never lifted. Its name is the simple key of its IRI, as for a Field."@en .
# A struct, a field and a data type are different kinds of thing: a field's value may be typed
# BY a struct, but no resource is both.
[] a owl:AllDisjointClasses ; owl:members ( :Struct :Field :DataType ) .
# The same disjointness as a validation rule: an OWL axiom is only a contradiction for a
# reasoner, so a description typing one node both a Struct and a Field would otherwise load.
:DisjointKindsShape a sh:NodeShape ; rdfs:label "Disjoint kinds shape"@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "A node is at most one of bddo:Struct, bddo:Field and bddo:DataType (subclasses included), as the owl:AllDisjointClasses axiom states."@en ;
    sh:targetClass :Struct , :Field , :DataType ;
    sh:sparql [ sh:message "A node cannot be both a {?a} and a {?b}: bddo:Struct, bddo:Field and bddo:DataType are disjoint." ;
        sh:prefixes :_prefixes ;
        sh:select """SELECT $this ?a ?b WHERE {
            $this rdf:type/rdfs:subClassOf* ?a , ?b .
            FILTER(?a IN (bddo:Struct, bddo:Field) && ?b IN (bddo:Field, bddo:DataType) && !sameTerm(?a, ?b))
        }""" ] .

# ---------- Delimited text records ----------
# Line- and character-delimited text: key = value headers, CSV, whitespace-separated grids.
# Records may nest, via bddo:hasGrouping, but only in the three token-delimited styles that
# vocabulary describes -- this is not a general grammar. A document with real recursive
# structure is a bddo:TreeDocument instead.
:DelimitedRecords a owl:Class ; rdfs:subClassOf :Struct ; rdfs:label "Delimited Records"@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "A Struct whose children are located by delimiter position rather than byte offset: a byte run split into records by recordDelimiter, each record optionally split into values by fieldDelimiter."@en .
:KeyValueHeader a owl:Class ; rdfs:subClassOf :DelimitedRecords ; rdfs:label "Key/Value Header"@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "DelimitedRecords whose records are key/value pairs split by keyValueSeparator. Each expected key is a Field carrying bddo:key, or, with no bddo:key, named by its simple key (the local name of its IRI after the last '.'); record order is not significant."@en .
:DelimitedTable a owl:Class ; rdfs:subClassOf :DelimitedRecords ; rdfs:label "Delimited Table"@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "DelimitedRecords whose records are rows with a fixed column sequence. The hasField list gives the columns in order; the row structure repeats until the byte run is exhausted."@en .
# ---------- Tree documents ----------
# XML and JSON labels wrap a payload that is otherwise already describable: PDS4, DIMAP, SAFE,
# SENTINEL-2, RadarSat-2, TerraSAR-X, CPHD and Zarr all pair a clean binary array with a
# manifest BDDO could not read, and the whole GML/KML/GeoJSON family is markup end to end.
#
# BDDO does not describe the SYNTAX of these documents -- it addresses nodes in the tree a
# conforming parser produces. That distinction is what keeps this from becoming an XML
# specification: the tree is someone else's output, and BDDO's job is only to say which node
# supplies which field.
:TreeDocument a owl:Class ; rdfs:subClassOf :Struct ; rdfs:label "Tree Document"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "A Struct whose children are located by a path into a parsed node tree rather than by byte offset or delimiter. The byte run is handed to a parser for the declared syntax; each Field names the node supplying its value."@en .
:TreeSyntax a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Tree Syntax"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "Which tree grammar a TreeDocument is written in, and therefore how its nodePath values are read."@en .
:NamespaceBinding a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Namespace Binding"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "A prefix-to-IRI binding for the paths of an XML TreeDocument."@en .

:RecordGrouping a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Record Grouping"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "How a DelimitedRecords container nests: which records open a group, which close it, and where the group's name comes from. A container without one is flat, which is what every DelimitedRecords was before nesting existed."@en .
:GroupingStyle a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Grouping Style"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "Which of the three ways a text format marks a nested group."@en .

:OffsetBase       a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Offset Base"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
:Encoding         a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Encoding"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
:ChecksumAlgorithm a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Checksum Algorithm"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
# Any CRC is fixed by the six parameters of the Rocksoft model, so one class covers every CRC a
# format uses without a new individual per variant. The named CRC individuals below carry the
# same six parameters, and their catalogue check value, as statements about themselves.
:CustomCrc a owl:Class ; rdfs:subClassOf :ChecksumAlgorithm ; rdfs:label "Custom CRC"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "A CRC stated by its Rocksoft-model parameters rather than named: bddo:crcWidth (8..64 bits), bddo:crcPolynomial, bddo:crcInit, bddo:crcReflectIn, bddo:crcReflectOut and bddo:crcXorOut, all six required. Used as the value of bddo:checksumAlgorithm for a CRC that no named algorithm identifies."@en .
# Union of DataType and Struct, used as the range of dataType / ruleDataType
:DataTypeOrStruct a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Data Type or Struct"@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ; owl:unionOf ( :DataType :Struct ) .

:hasArgument a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "has argument"@en ; rdfs:range rdf:List ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;

    rdfs:comment "Byte that quotes a value, allowing it to contain the field or record delimiter. A doubled quote inside a quoted value denotes a literal quote."@en .
:escapeChar             a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "escape character"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:hexBinary ;
    rdfs:comment "Byte that escapes the following byte, suppressing its delimiter or quote meaning."@en .
:commentPrefix          a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "comment prefix"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    rdfs:comment "A record beginning with this string (after optional whitespace) is ignored."@en .
:skipRecords            a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "skip records"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:nonNegativeInteger ;
    rdfs:comment "Number of leading records to discard before parsing, e.g. a CSV column-name row. Default 0."@en .
:whitespaceSeparated    a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "whitespace separated"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:boolean ;
    rdfs:comment "If true, values are separated by a RUN of one or more whitespace bytes (space, tab, CR, LF) rather than by a fixed bddo:fieldDelimiter. A run of any length separates exactly two values, so consecutive whitespace never yields an empty value, and leading or trailing whitespace on a record is ignored. This is what column-aligned ASCII grids need -- 'ncols        100', or a row of numbers padded to line up -- which a fixed delimiter cannot express. Mutually exclusive with bddo:fieldDelimiter. When bddo:recordDelimiter is also absent the content is one flat stream of whitespace-separated values rather than rows, which is how an ASCII grid whose rows wrap across lines is read; the grid's shape then comes from the field's dlv:DataLayout or repeat count, not from line breaks."@en .
:trimWhitespace         a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "trim whitespace"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:boolean ;
    rdfs:comment "If true, leading and trailing whitespace is removed from every key and value. Default false."@en .
# ---------- Tree document properties ----------
:treeSyntax a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "tree syntax"@en ; rdfs:range :TreeSyntax ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The grammar of a TreeDocument. Required, because it decides how every nodePath in the container is read."@en .
:xml a owl:NamedIndividual, :TreeSyntax ; rdfs:label "XML"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "Paths are ABBREVIATED XPATH: child steps, attribute steps (@name), and positional predicates ([n], 1-based). A deliberately small subset -- no axes, no functions, no comparison predicates -- because a description states where a value IS, and a path that can compute is a query engine's problem rather than a description's."@en .
:json a owl:NamedIndividual, :TreeSyntax ; rdfs:label "JSON"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "Paths are JSON POINTERS as defined by RFC 6901: /object/member, array indices as /list/0 (0-based). Adopted rather than invented, so its escaping rules for '/' and '~' inside a member name are already specified and already implemented."@en .

:nodePath a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "node path"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "Locates the node supplying this field's value, read according to the container's treeSyntax. A path selecting no node leaves the field absent; a path selecting several is an error unless the field repeats, in which case the field's value is the selected nodes in document order."@en .
:hasNamespaceBinding a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "has namespace binding"@en ; rdfs:range :NamespaceBinding ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "Binds the prefixes used in this document's paths. XML only, and not optional in practice: PDS4, DIMAP and SAFE are all namespaced, and an unprefixed path against a namespaced document silently matches nothing rather than failing."@en .
:namespacePrefix a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "namespace prefix"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The prefix as used in this container's nodePath values. The empty string binds the DEFAULT namespace, which an XPath subset cannot otherwise reach."@en .
:namespaceIRI a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "namespace IRI"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:anyURI ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The namespace the prefix stands for, matched against the document's own declarations rather than against the prefix it happens to use -- a document may spell the same namespace with any prefix."@en .

# ---------- Nested records ----------
# The flat key/value model reaches ESRI .hdr, ENVI and their kin, but stops at the label formats
# that are otherwise the same shape: PVL/ODL (PDS, ISIS2, ISIS3, VICAR) nests with
# OBJECT = ... END_OBJECT, ERMapper with <Name> Begin ... <Name> End, and INI-style labels with
# bracketed section headers. Refusing to nest kept nine drivers at payload-only for want of a
# closing token.
#
# Three styles rather than one general grammar. Each is fully described by two tokens plus where
# the group's NAME comes from, and that is the whole of it -- a general nested-grammar surface
# would be a parser generator, and this is not one.
:hasGrouping a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "has grouping"@en ; rdfs:range :RecordGrouping ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The nesting rule of a DelimitedRecords container. Absent means flat."@en .
:groupingStyle a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "grouping style"@en ; rdfs:range :GroupingStyle ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
:groupOpenToken a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "group open token"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The literal that marks a record as opening a group: the key OBJECT, the value Begin, or the bracket [ -- read according to groupingStyle."@en .
:groupCloseToken a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "group close token"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The literal that closes a group: END_OBJECT, End, or the bracket ]. For sectionHeaded it is the closing bracket of the header rather than a separate record, since a section is ended by the next one."@en .

:keyedGroup a owl:NamedIndividual, :GroupingStyle ; rdfs:label "keyed group"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "A record whose KEY equals groupOpenToken opens a group, and its VALUE names it; a record whose key equals groupCloseToken closes the innermost open group. PVL/ODL: OBJECT = IMAGE ... END_OBJECT = IMAGE. The closing record MAY repeat the name; a description need not say so, because the name is already known from the opening record."@en .
:valuedGroup a owl:NamedIndividual, :GroupingStyle ; rdfs:label "valued group"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "The mirror image: a record whose VALUE equals groupOpenToken opens a group, and its KEY names it. ERMapper: CoordinateSpace Begin ... CoordinateSpace End."@en .
:sectionHeaded a owl:NamedIndividual, :GroupingStyle ; rdfs:label "section headed"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "A record consisting of groupOpenToken, a name, and groupCloseToken opens a group and implicitly closes the previous one; the last runs to the end of the container. INI-style [Section] headers. Nesting is therefore FLAT ONE DEEP -- sections do not contain sections -- which is what the style actually permits, not a limitation of this model."@en .

:keyPath a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "key path"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "Locates a field inside nested groups, as group names and finally the key, joined by pathSeparator: 'IMAGE/BAND/NAME'. Mutually exclusive with bddo:key, which locates a field in a flat container -- a field cannot be found two ways, and a description that said both would leave a reader to guess which wins."@en .
:pathSeparator a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "path separator"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The separator used in this container's keyPath values. Default '/'. Declared on the container rather than repeated per field, and needed because a format whose group names may contain '/' has to choose something else."@en .

:key                    a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "key"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    rdfs:comment "The key naming this Field within a KeyValueHeader. Replaces positional location: the Field takes the value of whichever record carries this key. A field of a flat KeyValueHeader that declares no bddo:key is located by its simple key instead."@en .
:keyIsCaseInsensitive   a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "key is case insensitive"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:boolean ;
    rdfs:comment "If true, bddo:key matching ignores ASCII case. Default false."@en .
:numericBase            a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "numeric base"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:positiveInteger ;
    rdfs:comment "Radix used to read an :asciiInteger field: 8, 10 or 16. Default 10."@en .
:bitLength              a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "bit length"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:positiveInteger .
:repeatCount            a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "repeat count"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:nonNegativeInteger .
:repeatCountFromField   a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "repeat count from field"@en ; rdfs:range :Field .
:repeatCountFromExpression a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "repeat count from expression"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string .
:repeatUntil            a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "repeat until"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string .
:separatedBy            a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "separated by"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    rdfs:comment "The element extent of a field -- repeated or scalar -- whose value is a number written as text, separated rather than sized or terminated: each element is preceded and followed by a run of separator bytes, and the run belongs to the element that owns it so the byte sequence can be rebuilt exactly. The only defined value is \"whitespace\" (space, tab, CR, LF). A separator is a RUN and a terminator is a SEQUENCE: between two delimiters of a delimited table an empty value is meaningful, whereas an empty run is not a value at all. Permitted only on a field whose data type is a number written as text. Because the LAST element's trailing run consumes every separator byte up to the field's own extent (or end of stream), a field declaring bddo:separatedBy must either be the last field read from its enclosing region, or be immediately followed by data that does not itself begin with a separator byte (space, tab, CR, LF) -- otherwise the trailing run consumes bytes that belong to the following field."@en .
:atOffset               a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "at offset"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:nonNegativeInteger .
:atOffsetFromField      a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "at offset from field"@en ; rdfs:range :Field .
:atOffsetFromExpression a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "at offset from expression"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string .
:offsetBase             a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "offset base"@en ; rdfs:range :OffsetBase .
:alignment              a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "alignment"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:positiveInteger .
:hasFixedValue          a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "has fixed value"@en .
:isPresentIf            a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "is present if"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string .
:valueFromExpression    a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "value from expression"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    rdfs:comment "A HEL expression giving the value of a derived, zero-byte field. The field consumes no bytes; its computed value is placed in the parse context under the field's name so later sizing, offset, presence, mapping and validation expressions may reference it. A field with bddo:valueFromExpression declares no bddo:dataType and no sizing, offset or bit-length; the result's type is that of the HEL expression."@en .
:validIf                a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "valid if"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    rdfs:comment "A HEL boolean expression that MUST hold once this field is parsed; if it evaluates false the parse fails with a validation error. Use for post-parse constraints such as a magic number or a bound on a count. Richer, graph-level constraints belong in SHACL at the semantic layer."@en .
:rootKeyFromField       a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "root key from field"@en ; rdfs:range :Field ;
    rdfs:comment "After this Field is parsed, its value is also bound in the ROOT parsing context under the key given by the named sibling Field's parsed value, if that key is not already bound (first occurrence wins). Lets a later record address an earlier one through its discriminator -- `root.<key>.<field>` -- when the earlier record sits inside a repeated sequence that HEL cannot search. A neutral generalisation of the chunk-type aliasing that several chunked container formats need."@en .
:enumeration            a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "enumeration"@en ; rdfs:range :Enumeration .
:checksum               a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "checksum"@en ; rdfs:range :Checksum .

# ---------- DataType / Rule / Enum / Checksum properties ----------
:baseType      a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "base type"@en ; rdfs:range :BaseType .
:bitWidth      a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "bit width"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:positiveInteger .
:isSigned      a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "is signed"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:boolean .
:xsdType       a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "xsd type"@en ; rdfs:range rdfs:Datatype ;
    rdfs:comment "The RDF literal datatype produced when a value of this DataType is emitted."@en .
:condition     a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "condition"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    # Not the same property as hexplain:condition: this one guards how BYTES are read, while
    # parsing; that one guards which RDF a parsed node is LIFTED to. They are evaluated at
    # different times, and a processor reads each only on its own rule classes.
    rdfs:seeAlso <https://hexplain.io/ns/core#condition> .
:ruleDataType  a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "rule data type"@en ; rdfs:range :DataTypeOrStruct .
:ruleEndianness a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "rule endianness"@en ; rdfs:range :Endianness ;
    rdfs:comment "The byte order selected by an EndiannessRule."@en .
:hasEnumValue  a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "has enum value"@en ; rdfs:range :EnumValue .
:enumRawValue  a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "enum raw value"@en .
:enumSymbol    a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "enum symbol"@en .
:enumIsFlags   a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "enum is flags"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:boolean ;
    rdfs:comment "If true, the Enumeration is a bit-flag set: each EnumValue's raw value is a bitmask, and every symbol whose bit(s) are set in the parsed value is emitted."@en .
:checksumAlgorithm a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "checksum algorithm"@en ; rdfs:range :ChecksumAlgorithm .
:coversFromField   a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "covers from field"@en ; rdfs:range :Field .
:coversToField     a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "covers to field"@en ; rdfs:range :Field .
# A single expression can only describe a range it computes whole, so a checksum whose start
# is a named field and whose end is computed had no way to say so -- it had to abandon the
# field reference and recompute that offset arithmetically too. Splitting coverage into two
# independent bounds removes that all-or-nothing choice; either end may be a field or an
# expression. Offsets are absolute byte positions in the same frame of reference a size or
# offset expression on this struct would see.
:coversFromExpression a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "covers from expression"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    rdfs:comment "HEL expression evaluating to the covered range's START byte offset, inclusive. Alternative to coversFromField for the same bound."@en .
:coversToExpression a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "covers to expression"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    rdfs:comment "HEL expression evaluating to the covered range's END byte offset, EXCLUSIVE (one past the last covered byte). Alternative to coversToField for the same bound -- note coversToField names a field whose last byte is included, so the two forms do not take the same number."@en .
:coversExpression  a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "covers expression"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    owl:deprecated true ; dcterms:isReplacedBy :coversFromExpression , :coversToExpression ;
    rdfs:comment "Deprecated: a single expression yielding the whole [start,end) range. Superseded by the coversFromExpression/coversToExpression pair, which states each bound separately and so can mix field and expression ends. Retained for descriptions written against earlier BDDO."@en .
# The Rocksoft CRC model. Required on a bddo:CustomCrc; also stated on the named CRC individuals,
# where they record what the name means (tools/test_crc_catalogue.py recomputes each check value).
:crcWidth a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "CRC width"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:positiveInteger ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The width of a CRC in bits, 8 to 64: the degree of its generator polynomial and the width of its register and result."@en .
:crcPolynomial a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "CRC polynomial"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:nonNegativeInteger ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The generator polynomial of a CRC without its top (x^width) term, in normal (unreflected) form, as a non-negative integer below 2^width: CRC-32's is 0x04C11DB7 (79764919), not the reflected 0xEDB88320."@en .
:crcInit a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "CRC initial value"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:nonNegativeInteger ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The value the CRC register holds before the first byte is processed, unreflected, below 2^width."@en .
:crcReflectIn a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "CRC reflect input"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:boolean ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "If true, each input byte is processed least-significant bit first (reflected); if false, most-significant bit first."@en .
:crcReflectOut a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "CRC reflect output"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:boolean ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "If true, the final register is bit-reversed over its width before the final XOR."@en .
:crcXorOut a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "CRC final XOR"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:nonNegativeInteger ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The value XORed into the (possibly reflected) final register to give the CRC, below 2^width."@en .
:crcCheck a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "CRC check value"@en ; rdfs:range xsd:nonNegativeInteger ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The CRC of the nine ASCII bytes \"123456789\" under the algorithm's parameters: the catalogue check value that identifies a CRC variant. Informative on a named algorithm; never required, and never read by a processor."@en .

# ---------- HEL expressions ----------
# An expression is RDF text. Typing it bddo:HelExpression lets a reader -- a person, an editor, a
# linter -- tell an expression from a name or a label without knowing which property holds it.
# The literal's value is still the string, so every expression-valued property keeps its
# xsd:string range and an untyped expression stays valid: the shapes accept either datatype.
# It is declared here rather than in core because bddo is the lowest layer that holds
# expressions and must load without core.
:HelExpression a rdfs:Datatype ; rdfs:subClassOf xsd:string ;
    rdfs:label "HEL expression"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The datatype of a literal whose text is a HEL expression. Its lexical space is the text of HEL expressions as the HEL specification defines it, and a literal's value is that text as a string: the value space is a subset of xsd:string's, so a bddo:HelExpression literal is a value of every property whose range is xsd:string. Authoring tools SHOULD type expressions with it; an expression typed xsd:string remains valid. The datatype does not check the text: whether it parses is for the HEL evaluator to say."@en .
:HelExpressionValueShape a sh:NodeShape ;
    rdfs:label "HEL expression value shape"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "A HEL expression literal: typed xsd:string, as existing descriptions write it, or bddo:HelExpression. Every expression-valued property's shapes use it in place of sh:datatype xsd:string."@en ;
    sh:or ( [ sh:datatype xsd:string ] [ sh:datatype :HelExpression ] ) .

# The HEL version a description's expressions are written against. HEL 1.x adds functions but
# never changes what a 1.0 expression means, so a marker lets an evaluator that implements 1.0
# notice a description written for 1.1 -- which may call a 1.1 function -- and say so.
:helVersion a owl:AnnotationProperty ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    rdfs:label "HEL version"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The HEL version, as major.minor (\"1.0\"), that the expressions of a format description are written against, stated at most once on the description's owl:Ontology. Optional: absent, it is the HEL version of the specification edition the description conforms to. An evaluator implementing an earlier minor version of the same major version can still evaluate every expression that calls no function it lacks; a description declaring a later version tells it why a function may be unknown."@en .
:HelVersionShape a sh:NodeShape ;
    rdfs:label "HEL version shape"@en ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    sh:targetSubjectsOf :helVersion ;
    sh:property [ sh:path :helVersion ; sh:datatype xsd:string ; sh:maxCount 1 ; sh:pattern "^(0|[1-9][0-9]*)\\.(0|[1-9][0-9]*)$" ;
        sh:message "bddo:helVersion is stated at most once, as an xsd:string major.minor version of two decimal numbers without leading zeros, such as \"1.0\"." ] .

# ---------- Deprecated pre-release terms ----------
# Profiles written before BDDO 1.0 still use these IRIs and conforming processors still read
# them, so they are declared rather than silently unknown. Each keeps its original meaning;
# new descriptions MUST use the replacement. They are never emitted by current tooling.
:hasEndianness a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "has endianness (deprecated)"@en ; rdfs:range :Endianness ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    owl:deprecated true ; dcterms:isReplacedBy :endianness ;
    rdfs:comment "Deprecated pre-release spelling of bddo:endianness, with the same meaning. A processor reading a description that states both uses bddo:endianness."@en .
:usesStruct a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "uses struct (deprecated)"@en ; rdfs:range :Struct ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    owl:deprecated true ;
    skos:historyNote "Deprecated before BDDO 1.0 without a replacement: splicing another struct's fields into this one's has no single equivalent. A description states the fields directly, or names the other struct as the bddo:dataType of a field, which nests its fields instead of splicing them."@en ;
    rdfs:comment "Deprecated pre-release template reference: the subject Struct's effective field list is the named Struct's fields followed by its own. There is no single replacement term; new descriptions state the fields directly, or compose with a Field whose bddo:dataType is the other Struct (which nests rather than splices)."@en .
:integer a owl:NamedIndividual, :BaseType ; rdfs:label "Base type Integer (deprecated)"@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    owl:deprecated true ; dcterms:isReplacedBy :baseInteger ;
    rdfs:comment "Deprecated pre-release name of bddo:baseInteger, with the same meaning."@en .
:float a owl:NamedIndividual, :BaseType ; rdfs:label "Base type Float (deprecated)"@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    owl:deprecated true ; dcterms:isReplacedBy :baseFloat ;
    rdfs:comment "Deprecated pre-release name of bddo:baseFloat, with the same meaning."@en .
